<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every closed connected oriented smooth manifold admitting strictly positive scalar curvature is rationally inessential: its rational fundamental class maps to zero under the classifying map. No spin or fundamental-group hypothesis is needed, and there is no upper dimension bound. This proves the Gromov–Lawson aspherical conjecture; moreover, every nonnegative-scalar-curvature metric on a closed aspherical manifold is flat. We also show that every closed oriented smooth manifold of positive dimension with nonnegative scalar curvature has zero real simplicial volume, proving the qualitative vanishing consequence of Gromov's conjectural comparison.

- **thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $M^n$ be a closed connected oriented smooth manifold of dimension $n\ge2$. If $M$ admits a Riemannian metric of strictly positive scalar curvature, then $$(c_M)_*[M]=0\qquad\text{in }H_n(B\pi_1(M);\mathbb Q).$$
  - Explanation: Strict positive scalar curvature forces the rational classifying image of the fundamental class to vanish.
- **cor-aspherical** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-aspherical)): No closed smooth aspherical manifold, whether orientable or not, admits a Riemannian metric of strictly positive scalar curvature.
  - Explanation: Closed smooth aspherical manifolds admit no strictly positive scalar curvature metric, including the nonorientable case.
- **cor-aspherical-flat** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-aspherical-flat)): Let $g$ be a Riemannian metric of nonnegative scalar curvature on a closed smooth aspherical manifold $M$, not necessarily oriented. Then $g$ is flat.
  - Explanation: The given nonnegative scalar curvature metric on a closed aspherical manifold must be flat.
- **cor-degree-aspherical** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-degree-aspherical)): Let $M^n$ and $N^n$ be closed connected oriented smooth manifolds of dimension $n\ge2$, with $N$ aspherical. If a continuous map $f:M\to N$ has nonzero ordinary oriented degree $d\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$, then $M$ admits no Riemannian metric whose scalar curvature is strictly positive everywhere.* *In particular, if $P^n$ is any closed connected oriented smooth manifold, the oriented connected sum $N\#P$ admits no such metric.
  - Explanation: A nonzero oriented degree map to a closed aspherical target obstructs strictly positive scalar curvature; the connected sum inherits this obstruction.
- **cor-simplicial** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-simplicial)): Every closed oriented smooth manifold of positive dimension admitting a Riemannian metric of nonnegative scalar curvature has zero real simplicial volume.
  - Explanation: Nonnegative scalar curvature forces real simplicial volume to vanish under the stated closedness and orientation assumptions.
- **lem-essentiality** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-essentiality)): If a closed connected oriented manifold $M^n$ has positive real simplicial volume, then $(c_M)_*[M]\ne0$ in $H_n(B\Gamma;\mathbb Q)$.
  - Explanation: Positive real simplicial volume detects a nonzero rational classifying image.
- **lem-scalar-reduction** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-reduction)): Let $M$ be closed and connected, not necessarily oriented, and suppose that $g$ has nonnegative scalar curvature. Either $M$ admits a metric of strictly positive scalar curvature, or $g$ is Ricci-flat. In the Ricci-flat case, if $M$ is oriented and has positive dimension, then $\\|M\\|=0$.
  - Explanation: A nonnegative scalar curvature metric yields either a strictly positive scalar curvature metric or Ricci flatness; the oriented positive-dimensional Ricci-flat branch has zero simplicial volume.
- **thm-comparison-data** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-comparison-data)): Let $(M^n,g_M)$ be a closed connected oriented Riemannian manifold, $n\ge2$, let $\Gamma=\pi_1(M)$, and suppose that its classifying map $c_M:M\to B\Gamma$ satisfies $$(c_M)_*[M]\ne0\quad\text{in }H_n(B\Gamma;\mathbb Q).$$ There are constants $J>0$ and $R_0\ge1$ such that, for every real $R\ge R_0$, there exist a closed oriented smooth manifold $A$, a principal left $\Gamma$-cover $\widehat A\to A$, and an integer $q=n+\dim A>n$ with the following properties. The diagonal cover $$Z=(\widetilde M\times\widehat A)/\Gamma\longrightarrow M\times A$$ is oriented and has dimension $q$. There are a smooth compactly supported map $G:Z\to\mathbb R^q$ and a relatively compact open set $\Omega\subset Z$ such that $$\label{eq:comparison-degree} \deg(G,\Omega,0)\ne0, \qquad \norm{G}_\infty>3\quad\text{on }\partial\Omega,$$ and $$\label{eq:comparison-bounds} \max_{1\le i\le q}\sup_Z\abs{d_{\widetilde M}G_i}_{g_M} \le\frac JR, \qquad \log q\le JR.$$ Write $\pi_Z:Z\to M\times A$ for the covering projection. Its differential gives a global splitting $$TZ=E_M\oplus E_A,\qquad E_M=(d\pi_Z)^{-1}(TM\oplus0),\qquad E_A=(d\pi_Z)^{-1}(0\oplus TA).$$ The bundle $E_M$ is tangent to the $\widetilde M$ fibres of $Z\to A$, and $E_A$ is the flat lift of the parameter directions. We write $d_{\widetilde M}G_i=dG_i\|_{E_M}$. For each fixed $R$, after choosing any smooth metric on $A$, all derivatives of $G$ in the lifted product geometry are bounded. No bound uniform in $R$ is asserted for parameter derivatives or for derivatives of order greater than one. The covers and the parameter manifolds need not be connected.
  - Explanation: A detected rational fundamental class supplies finite comparison coordinates with local degree and controlled fibre derivatives; parameter control is asserted only at fixed scale.
- **lem-probability-map** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-probability-map)): There are $s\ge1$, $H<\infty$, a smooth equivariant probability map $h:\widetilde M\to\mathcal K_s$, and an equivariant simplicial map $h_0:\widetilde M\to\mathcal K_s$ such that* 1. *the coordinate functions of $h$ are smooth and locally supported on finitely many labels, and $\abs{dh}_{\ell^1}\le H$;* 2. *$h$ and $h_0$ are joined by an equivariant homotopy that is proper as a map $\widetilde M\times[0,1]\to\mathcal K_s$;* 3. *there is an invariant alternating homogeneous rational group $n$-cocycle $\omega$ whose evaluation on the labelled fundamental simplices of $M$ is nonzero.
  - Explanation: Equivariant probability maps carry the detecting cocycle while preserving properness of the comparison homotopy.
- **lem-parameter-thickening** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-parameter-thickening)): Put $v_0=\dim\mathcal O_L$. There are a closed oriented smooth $d$-manifold $C$, with $d\le2v_0+2$, a principal left $\Gamma$-cover $\widehat C\to C$, and equivariant maps $$\mathcal O_L\xrightarrow{i}\widehat C \xrightarrow{r}\mathcal K_L, \qquad r i=\pi.$$ Moreover $v_0\le\abs{B_\Gamma(1,L)}$.
  - Explanation: An ordered auxiliary complex admits a finite smooth parameter thickening and a free principal cover even in the presence of torsion.
- **lem-dual-carrier** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-dual-carrier)): One can triangulate $C$ so finely that the lifted triangulation has the following properties:* 1. *the union of the supports of $r(o)$ over any closed primal simplex has diameter at most $2L$;* 2. *there is an augmentation-preserving equivariant chain map $$\mathcal F:C_*(\mathcal O_L)\longrightarrow C_*^{\mathrm{dual}}(\widehat C)$$ with finite output, such that if a dual cell to a primal simplex $\tau$ occurs in $\mathcal F(e)$, then every label of every $r(o)$, $o\in\tau$, is within $2L$ of every vertex of $e$.
  - Explanation: A fine triangulation supplies an equivariant finite-output dual-chain map with controlled label distances.
- **lem-controlled-cap** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-controlled-cap)): There are equivariant linear maps with finite output $$\label{eq:cap-chain-map} \begin{gathered} T_j:C_j(\mathcal K_L;\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow C_{j-n}^{\mathrm{dual}}(\widehat C;\mathbb Q),\qquad j\ge0,\\ \partial T_j=(-1)^nT_{j-1}\partial,\qquad j\ge1, \end{gathered}$$ where chain groups in negative degrees are zero. For every oriented $n$-simplex $e$, the augmentation of $T_n(e)$ is $\omega(e)$. For every oriented $j$-simplex $e$, if a dual cell to a primal simplex $\tau$ occurs in $T_j(e)$, then every label of $r(o)$, for every $o\in\tau$, is within $3L$ of every vertex of $e$.
  - Explanation: The detecting cocycle defines a controlled cap operation with the required boundary sign and augmentation.
- **prop-compact-pairing** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-compact-pairing)): There are a compact set $K_*\subset D_L$ and a class $$u\in H^{n+d}(D_L,D_L\setminus K_*;\mathbb Q)$$ such that* 1. *if $[t,o]\in K_*$, every label of $t$ is within $7L$ of every label of $r(o)$;* 2. *the compactly supported evaluation $$\label{eq:compact-pairing-nonzero} \ip{\Phi_h^*u,[X]_{\mathrm{lf}}}\ne0$$ is nonzero.* *Here $[X]_{\mathrm{lf}}$ is the locally finite fundamental class.
  - Explanation: The controlled cap operation gives a compactly supported cohomology class with nonzero locally finite pairing.
- **lem-distance-profiles** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-distance-profiles)): The map $V:D_L\to\mathbb R^q$ has the following properties.* 1. *It is continuous and proper, and $\norm{V}_{p_R}\le24L$ on $K_*$.* 2. *If $t,t'\in\mathcal K_s$ satisfy $$\max\{\norm{V_o(t)}_\infty,\norm{V_o(t')}_\infty\}\le110L, \qquad \norm{V_o(t)-V_o(t')}_\infty\le14R,$$ then the union of their supports has diameter at most $L$.* 3. *The map $\mathcal V=V\Phi_h:X\to\mathbb R^q$ is smooth. With $K_0=sH$, each of its rows satisfies $$\sup_X\abs{d_{\widetilde M}\mathcal V_i}\le K_0,$$ where $K_0$ is independent of $R$.
  - Explanation: Distance profiles are proper, preserve support control under nearby profiles, and have scale-independent fibre derivative bounds.
- **lem-relative-degree** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-relative-degree)): There is a compact oriented smooth $(q-n)$-manifold $A_+$ with boundary, together with a smooth map $$(o_0,y_0):A_+\longrightarrow C\times\mathbb R^q,$$ such that $\norm{y_0}_{p_R}<T_0$ everywhere, $\rho(o_0,y_0)>4R$ on $\partial A_+$, and its intersections with $j:N\to C\times\mathbb R^q$ are transverse, interior, and have nonzero total oriented count. The component maps may be taken constant in the inward collar coordinate near $\partial A_+$.
  - Explanation: Relative duality and rational realization produce a parameter cycle whose transverse intersections have nonzero signed count.
- **prop-scalar-update** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-scalar-update)): At a point where $t>0$, choose a $g$-orthonormal frame $e_1=\nabla f/t,e_2,\ldots,e_{\dim B}$, and put $$\mathfrak p=\frac{tN'(t)}{N(t)},\qquad A=d\log t,\qquad C_{jk}=t^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_g f(e_j,e_k)\quad(j,k>1).$$ Then $$\label{eq:scalar-exact} \begin{split} \mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_{g^+}-\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g ={}&D^2-2U(D)+r^2\abs{C}^2+(e_1r)^2\\ &+2r^2(1+\mathfrak p)(v^2-\mathfrak p r^2) \sum_{j>1}A(e_j)^2. \end{split}$$ In particular, the final term is nonnegative whenever $\mathfrak p\ge0$ and $\mathfrak p N(t)^2t^2\le1$. For $N\equiv1$, one also has, everywhere, $$\label{eq:ordinary-scalar-update} \mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_{g^+}-\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g=\abs{II}^2+D^2-2U(D),$$ where $II$ is the second fundamental form of the ordinary product graph. The inverse graph metric on base covectors and the volume form of $g^+$ are $$\label{eq:graph-inverse-volume} P=g^{-1}-U\otimes U, \qquad dV_{g^+}=dV_g\,d\theta.$$
  - Explanation: The graph and reciprocal circle update has an exact scalar identity, an inverse-metric decrease, and a compensating volume identity.
- **lem-uniform-linear** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-linear)): Suppose that, in the reference charts, a scalar operator has the form $$L\xi=-a^{ij}\partial_i\partial_j\xi+b^i\partial_i\xi+c\xi,$$ with uniformly $C^{0,\alpha}$ coefficients, uniformly bounded drift, uniformly positive definite principal matrix, and $c\ge c_0>0$. Then $$L:C_b^{2,\alpha}(B)\longrightarrow C_b^{0,\alpha}(B)$$ is an isomorphism. Its inverse is bounded in terms of the indicated uniform bounds. If the coefficients have bounded derivatives of every order, the inverse also maps $C_b^{j,\alpha}$ boundedly into $C_b^{j+2,\alpha}$ for every $j\ge0$.
  - Explanation: Uniform ellipticity and a positive zeroth-order term give a bounded inverse on uniform Hölder spaces.
- **thm-uniform-graph** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-uniform-graph)): Let $(B,g)$ be uniformly controlled as above, and let $\ell\in C_b^\infty(B)$. Fix $\varepsilon,w,B_0,s>0$ and $0\le\beta<1$. Suppose $N$ is smooth, positive, nondecreasing, constant near zero, and equal to $1$ for $t\ge1/2$. Define the quantities in <a href="#eq:graph-quantities" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:graph-quantities">[eq:graph-quantities]</a> and $$\lambda(r)=B_0(r^2+s^2)^{\beta/2}.$$ There is a unique solution in the uniform $C^{2,\alpha}$ class with a uniform band margin to $$\label{eq:graph-equation} -\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_g U =\lambda(r)\tan\left(\frac{\ell-\varepsilon f}{w}\right), \qquad \abs{\ell-\varepsilon f}<\frac{\pi w}{2}.$$ This solution lies in $C_b^\infty(B)$, and the band has a positive uniform margin. The metric <a href="#eq:graph-metric-update" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:graph-metric-update">[eq:graph-metric-update]</a> is uniformly controlled and complete.* *The solution is invariant under every isometry preserving the equation’s data. The following parameter statement will be used below. Fix the reference cover, its metric and nested charts, and the equation parameters $\varepsilon,w,B_0,s,\beta,N$. Let $(g_a,\ell_a)$ be a jointly smooth family over a finite-dimensional parameter manifold $A$. For every relatively compact subset $V'\Subset V$ of a parameter chart, assume common constants $c_{V'},C_{V'}>0$ such that $$c_{V'}g_{\rm ref}\le g_a\le C_{V'}g_{\rm ref}\qquad(a\in V'),$$ and common bounds in the fixed reference charts for every mixed derivative of the coefficients of $g_a$ and of $\ell_a$, including order zero. Then $a\mapsto f_a$ is smooth in every $C_b^{j,\alpha}$ norm, for $j\ge0$ and $0<\alpha<1$. If $A$ is compact, all mixed derivatives of $f_a$ are uniformly bounded and the tangent band has a common positive margin. For a bundle of such covers, the local solutions glue whenever fixed reference deck transformations carry one local data set to another.
  - Explanation: The nonlinear graph equation has a unique uniformly smooth solution with a positive band margin, symmetry, and parameter compatibility.
- **lem-one-pass** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-one-pass)): Let $q\ge2$ and $L,w>0$. On a uniformly controlled metric of the form <a href="#eq:fibre-stabilized-metric" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fibre-stabilized-metric">[eq:fibre-stabilized-metric]</a>, suppose smooth bounded torus-invariant functions $\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_q$ have bounded derivatives of every order. For a smooth family over a compact parameter manifold, assume in each of finitely many parameter charts common bounds for every mixed fibre and parameter derivative of the metric coefficients, warps, and functions, and a common equivalence to the reference fibre product. Suppose $$\abs{d\ell_i}_{g_*}\le L,$$ where $g_*$ denotes the metric on $E_M$ at the start of the pass. Then one can add $q$ circles and replace these functions by $\ell_i^+$ so that $$\label{eq:pass-function-bounds} \abs{\ell_i^+-\ell_i}<\frac\pi2w, \qquad \abs{d\ell_i^+}_{g_h^+}\le\frac L4, \qquad g_h^+\ge g_*.$$ For an absolute constant $C_0$, the full fibre scalar curvature satisfies $$\label{eq:pass-scalar-bound} \mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_{g^+}\ge\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g -C_0(n+1)(2+\log q)\frac{L^2}{w^2}.$$ All data at the new finite stage remain uniformly controlled. For a compact parameter family satisfying the stated hypotheses, the new mixed derivatives are also uniformly bounded.
  - Explanation: Processing the coordinates shortens their fibre differentials while the scalar loss depends logarithmically on the coordinate count.
- **lem-parameter-scaling** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-parameter-scaling)): Fix a finite-stage smooth family of metrics of the form <a href="#eq:fibre-stabilized-metric" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fibre-stabilized-metric">[eq:fibre-stabilized-metric]</a>, equivariant under the flat transition maps over the closed manifold $A$, with all mixed derivatives bounded and uniform equivalence to a reference product. For any fixed metric $g_A$ on $A$, set $$g_P=g_h+P^2g_A+\sum_{\nu=1}^b m_\nu^2d\theta_\nu^2 \quad\text{on }Z\times\mathbb T^b,$$ using the flat splitting and no mixed parameter terms. As $P\to\infty$, $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_{g_P}$ converges uniformly to the scalar curvature of the fibre metrics. If $H:Z\to\mathbb R^q$ has bounded mixed derivatives, its differential in the parameter directions has norm tending uniformly to zero. Each $g_P$ is complete and uniformly controlled.
  - Explanation: At a fixed finite stage, magnifying the parameter metric suppresses parameter differential norms and makes scalar curvature approach the fibre value.
- **thm-shortening** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-shortening)): Suppose $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_M\ge\kappa>0$. On the above bundle $Z$, let $G:Z\to\mathbb R^q$ be smooth and bounded with all mixed derivatives bounded, and suppose $L_0>0$ and $$\sup_{1\le i\le q}\abs{d_{\widetilde M}G_i}\le L_0, \qquad q\ge2.$$ There is a constant $C_n$ depending only on $n$ such that, if $$\label{eq:shortening-smallness} C_n(2+\log q)L_0^2\le\kappa/4,$$ then for every finite integer $k\ge1$ there are a complete stabilized metric $$\mathfrak g_k=h_k+\sum_{\nu=1}^{kq}m_{k,\nu}^2d\theta_\nu^2 \quad\text{on }Z\times\mathbb T^{kq}$$ and a smooth bounded map $G^{(k)}:Z\to\mathbb R^q$ satisfying $$\label{eq:shortening-output} \mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_{\mathfrak g_k}\ge\kappa/2,\qquad \norm{G^{(k)}-G}_{\infty}<\pi/10,\qquad \abs{dG^{(k)}}_{\rm op}\le\sqrt{q+1}\,4^{-k}L_0.$$ The infinity norm in the middle inequality is the target coordinate maximum, followed by the supremum over $Z$. All metric coefficients, warps, and maps have uniform bounds of every order at each fixed finite stage; their equivalence and derivative constants may depend on that stage.
  - Explanation: Finite passes preserve a positive stabilized scalar bound while controlling total coordinate displacement and the full base differential.
- **cor-stabilized-contraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-stabilized-contraction)): Assume $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_M\ge\kappa>0$ and take the comparison data of <a href="#thm:comparison-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:comparison-data">8</a> at scale $R$, so that $$L_0=J/R,\qquad \log q\le JR.$$ For all sufficiently large $R$, fix these data once and for all. Then for every finite $k\ge1$ there is a stabilized metric $\mathfrak g_k$ on $Z\times\mathbb T^{kq}$ with scalar curvature at least $\kappa/2$, and a smooth map $$F_k:Z\longrightarrow S^q$$ equal to a single value outside a compact set, with nonzero degree, such that $$\label{eq:stabilized-contraction-bound} \abs{dF_k}_{\rm op}\le C(q)4^{-k}J/R.$$ The sphere has its standard unit metric. The constant $C(q)$ is independent of $k$. If desired, the circle lengths may be increased so that the projection to the standard $\mathbb T^{kq}$ also has operator norm at most $4^{-k}J/R$; this does not change scalar curvature or the bound on $dF_k$.
  - Explanation: Once comparison scale and base dimension are fixed, finite shortening yields compactly supported nonzero-degree sphere maps with decaying differential norms.
- **thm-torical-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-torical-obstruction)): For every integer $d\geq2$ and every $\chi>0$ there is a number $\delta(d,\chi)>0$ for which the following data do not exist:* 1. *a smooth oriented $d$-manifold $Y$ without boundary, possibly disconnected, with a torus-stabilized metric $\mathcal G$ satisfying $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_{\mathcal G}\geq\chi$;* 2. *a smooth map $F:Y\to S^d$, equal to one value outside a compact set, such that $\deg F\ne0$ and $\norm{dF}_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta(d,\chi)$ everywhere.* *The constant is independent of the finite circle count in <a href="#eq:stabilized-metric" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:stabilized-metric">[eq:stabilized-metric]</a>. Neither the base metric nor the stabilized metric is assumed complete.
  - Explanation: A positive stabilized scalar lower bound obstructs sufficiently contracting nonzero-degree sphere maps independently of the finite circle count.

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-comparison-data",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(M^n,g_M)$ be a closed connected oriented Riemannian manifold, $n\\ge2$, let $\\Gamma=\\pi_1(M)$, and suppose that its classifying map $c_M:M\\to B\\Gamma$ satisfies $$(c_M)_*[M]\\ne0\\quad\\text{in }H_n(B\\Gamma;\\mathbb Q).$$ There are constants $J>0$ and $R_0\\ge1$ such that, for every real $R\\ge R_0$, there exist a closed oriented smooth manifold $A$, a principal left $\\Gamma$-cover $\\widehat A\\to A$, and an integer $q=n+\\dim A>n$ with the following properties.",

        },

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-comparison-data",
          "exact_quote": "for every real $R\\ge R_0$",

        }

    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-probability-map",
        "exact_quote": "There are $s\\ge1$, $H<\\infty$, a smooth equivariant probability map $h:\\widetilde M\\to\\mathcal K_s$, and an equivariant simplicial map $h_0:\\widetilde M\\to\\mathcal K_s$ such that*\n\n1.  *the coordinate functions of $h$ are smooth and locally supported on finitely many labels, and $\\abs{dh}_{\\ell^1}\\le H$;*\n\n2.  *$h$ and $h_0$ are joined by an equivariant homotopy that is proper as a map $\\widetilde M\\times[0,1]\\to\\mathcal K_s$;*\n\n3.  *there is an invariant alternating homogeneous rational group $n$-cocycle $\\omega$ whose evaluation on the labelled fundamental simplices of $M$ is nonzero.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-dual-carrier",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-controlled-cap",
        "exact_quote": "There are equivariant linear maps with finite output $$\\label{eq:cap-chain-map}\n \\begin{gathered}\n T_j:C_j(\\mathcal K_L;\\mathbb Q)\\longrightarrow\n C_{j-n}^{\\mathrm{dual}}(\\widehat C;\\mathbb Q),\\qquad j\\ge0,\\\\\n \\partial T_j=(-1)^nT_{j-1}\\partial,\\qquad j\\ge1,\n \\end{gathered}$$ where chain groups in negative degrees are zero.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar-update",
        "exact_quote": "At a point where $t>0$, choose a $g$-orthonormal frame $e_1=\\nabla f/t,e_2,\\ldots,e_{\\dim B}$, and put $$\\mathfrak p=\\frac{tN'(t)}{N(t)},\\qquad\n A=d\\log t,\\qquad\n C_{jk}=t^{-1}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}_g f(e_j,e_k)\\quad(j,k>1).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:scalar-exact}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_{g^+}-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_g\n ={}&D^2-2U(D)+r^2\\abs{C}^2+(e_1r)^2\\\\\n &+2r^2(1+\\mathfrak p)(v^2-\\mathfrak p r^2)\n       \\sum_{j>1}A(e_j)^2.\n \\end{split}$$ In particular, the final term is nonnegative whenever $\\mathfrak p\\ge0$ and $\\mathfrak p N(t)^2t^2\\le1$. For $N\\equiv1$, one also has, everywhere, $$\\label{eq:ordinary-scalar-update}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_{g^+}-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_g=\\abs{II}^2+D^2-2U(D),$$ where $II$ is the second fundamental form of the ordinary product graph. The inverse graph metric on base covectors and the volume form of $g^+$ are $$\\label{eq:graph-inverse-volume}\n P=g^{-1}-U\\otimes U,\n \\qquad dV_{g^+}=dV_g\\,d\\theta.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-335.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar-update",
          "exact_quote": "At a point where $t>0$, choose a $g$-orthonormal frame $e_1=\\nabla f/t,e_2,\\ldots,e_{\\dim B}$, and put $$\\mathfrak p=\\frac{tN'(t)}{N(t)},\\qquad\n A=d\\log t,\\qquad\n C_{jk}=t^{-1}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}_g f(e_j,e_k)\\quad(j,k>1).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:scalar-exact}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_{g^+}-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_g\n ={}&D^2-2U(D)+r^2\\abs{C}^2+(e_1r)^2\\\\\n &+2r^2(1+\\mathfrak p)(v^2-\\mathfrak p r^2)\n       \\sum_{j>1}A(e_j)^2.\n \\end{split}$$ In particular, the final term is nonnegative whenever $\\mathfrak p\\ge0$ and $\\mathfrak p N(t)^2t^2\\le1$. For $N\\equiv1$, one also has, everywhere, $$\\label{eq:ordinary-scalar-update}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_{g^+}-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_g=\\abs{II}^2+D^2-2U(D),$$ where $II$ is the second fundamental form of the ordinary product graph. The inverse graph metric on base covectors and the volume form of $g^+$ are $$\\label{eq:graph-inverse-volume}\n P=g^{-1}-U\\otimes U,\n \\qquad dV_{g^+}=dV_g\\,d\\theta.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-335.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "At a point where $t>0$, choose a $g$-orthonormal frame $e_1=\\nabla f/t,e_2,\\ldots,e_{\\dim B}$, and put $$\\mathfrak p=\\frac{tN'(t)}{N(t)},\\qquad\n A=d\\log t,\\qquad\n C_{jk}=t^{-1}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}_g f(e_j,e_k)\\quad(j,k>1).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:scalar-exact}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_{g^+}-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_g\n ={}&D^2-2U(D)+r^2\\abs{C}^2+(e_1r)^2\\\\\n &+2r^2(1+\\mathfrak p)(v^2-\\mathfrak p r^2)\n       \\sum_{j>1}A(e_j)^2.\n \\end{split}$$ In particular, the final term is nonnegative whenever $\\mathfrak p\\ge0$ and $\\mathfrak p N(t)^2t^2\\le1$. For $N\\equiv1$, one also has, everywhere, $$\\label{eq:ordinary-scalar-update}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_{g^+}-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_g=\\abs{II}^2+D^2-2U(D),$$ where $II$ is the second fundamental form of the ordinary product graph. The inverse graph metric on base covectors and the volume form of $g^+$ are $$\\label{eq:graph-inverse-volume}\n P=g^{-1}-U\\otimes U,\n \\qquad dV_{g^+}=dV_g\\,d\\theta.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-uniform-linear",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that, in the reference charts, a scalar operator has the form $$L\\xi=-a^{ij}\\partial_i\\partial_j\\xi+b^i\\partial_i\\xi+c\\xi,$$ with uniformly $C^{0,\\alpha}$ coefficients, uniformly bounded drift, uniformly positive definite principal matrix, and $c\\ge c_0>0$. Then $$L:C_b^{2,\\alpha}(B)\\longrightarrow C_b^{0,\\alpha}(B)$$ is an isomorphism. Its inverse is bounded in terms of the indicated uniform bounds. If the coefficients have bounded derivatives of every order, the inverse also maps $C_b^{j,\\alpha}$ boundedly into $C_b^{j+2,\\alpha}$ for every $j\\ge0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-uniform-graph",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(B,g)$ be uniformly controlled as above, and let $\\ell\\in C_b^\\infty(B)$. Fix $\\varepsilon,w,B_0,s>0$ and $0\\le\\beta<1$. Suppose $N$ is smooth, positive, nondecreasing, constant near zero, and equal to $1$ for $t\\ge1/2$. Define the quantities in <a href=\"#eq:graph-quantities\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:graph-quantities\">[eq:graph-quantities]</a> and $$\\lambda(r)=B_0(r^2+s^2)^{\\beta/2}.$$ There is a unique solution in the uniform $C^{2,\\alpha}$ class with a uniform band margin to $$\\label{eq:graph-equation}\n -\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_g U\n   =\\lambda(r)\\tan\\left(\\frac{\\ell-\\varepsilon f}{w}\\right),\n \\qquad \\abs{\\ell-\\varepsilon f}<\\frac{\\pi w}{2}.$$ This solution lies in $C_b^\\infty(B)$, and the band has a positive uniform margin. The metric <a href=\"#eq:graph-metric-update\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:graph-metric-update\">[eq:graph-metric-update]</a> is uniformly controlled and complete.*\n\n*The solution is invariant under every isometry preserving the equation’s data. The following parameter statement will be used below. Fix the reference cover, its metric and nested charts, and the equation parameters $\\varepsilon,w,B_0,s,\\beta,N$. Let $(g_a,\\ell_a)$ be a jointly smooth family over a finite-dimensional parameter manifold $A$. For every relatively compact subset $V'\\Subset V$ of a parameter chart, assume common constants $c_{V'},C_{V'}>0$ such that $$c_{V'}g_{\\rm ref}\\le g_a\\le C_{V'}g_{\\rm ref}\\qquad(a\\in V'),$$ and common bounds in the fixed reference charts for every mixed derivative of the coefficients of $g_a$ and of $\\ell_a$, including order zero. Then $a\\mapsto f_a$ is smooth in every $C_b^{j,\\alpha}$ norm, for $j\\ge0$ and $0<\\alpha<1$. If $A$ is compact, all mixed derivatives of $f_a$ are uniformly bounded and the tangent band has a common positive margin. For a bundle of such covers, the local solutions glue whenever fixed reference deck transformations carry one local data set to another.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-one-pass",
        "exact_quote": "Let $q\\ge2$ and $L,w>0$. On a uniformly controlled metric of the form <a href=\"#eq:fibre-stabilized-metric\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fibre-stabilized-metric\">[eq:fibre-stabilized-metric]</a>, suppose smooth bounded torus-invariant functions $\\ell_1,\\ldots,\\ell_q$ have bounded derivatives of every order. For a smooth family over a compact parameter manifold, assume in each of finitely many parameter charts common bounds for every mixed fibre and parameter derivative of the metric coefficients, warps, and functions, and a common equivalence to the reference fibre product. Suppose $$\\abs{d\\ell_i}_{g_*}\\le L,$$ where $g_*$ denotes the metric on $E_M$ at the start of the pass. Then one can add $q$ circles and replace these functions by $\\ell_i^+$ so that $$\\label{eq:pass-function-bounds}\n \\abs{\\ell_i^+-\\ell_i}<\\frac\\pi2w,\n \\qquad \\abs{d\\ell_i^+}_{g_h^+}\\le\\frac L4,\n \\qquad g_h^+\\ge g_*.$$ For an absolute constant $C_0$, the full fibre scalar curvature satisfies $$\\label{eq:pass-scalar-bound}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_{g^+}\\ge\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_g\n       -C_0(n+1)(2+\\log q)\\frac{L^2}{w^2}.$$ All data at the new finite stage remain uniformly controlled. For a compact parameter family satisfying the stated hypotheses, the new mixed derivatives are also uniformly bounded.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite-stage smooth family of metrics of the form <a href=\"#eq:fibre-stabilized-metric\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fibre-stabilized-metric\">[eq:fibre-stabilized-metric]</a>, equivariant under the flat transition maps over the closed manifold $A$, with all mixed derivatives bounded and uniform equivalence to a reference product.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-shortening",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_M\\ge\\kappa>0$. On the above bundle $Z$, let $G:Z\\to\\mathbb R^q$ be smooth and bounded with all mixed derivatives bounded, and suppose $L_0>0$ and $$\\sup_{1\\le i\\le q}\\abs{d_{\\widetilde M}G_i}\\le L_0,\n \\qquad q\\ge2.$$ There is a constant $C_n$ depending only on $n$ such that, if $$\\label{eq:shortening-smallness}\n C_n(2+\\log q)L_0^2\\le\\kappa/4,$$ then for every finite integer $k\\ge1$ there are a complete stabilized metric $$\\mathfrak g_k=h_k+\\sum_{\\nu=1}^{kq}m_{k,\\nu}^2d\\theta_\\nu^2\n \\quad\\text{on }Z\\times\\mathbb T^{kq}$$ and a smooth bounded map $G^{(k)}:Z\\to\\mathbb R^q$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:shortening-output}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_{\\mathfrak g_k}\\ge\\kappa/2,\\qquad\n \\norm{G^{(k)}-G}_{\\infty}<\\pi/10,\\qquad\n \\abs{dG^{(k)}}_{\\rm op}\\le\\sqrt{q+1}\\,4^{-k}L_0.$$ The infinity norm in the middle inequality is the target coordinate maximum, followed by the supremum over $Z$. All metric coefficients, warps, and maps have uniform bounds of every order at each fixed finite stage; their equivalence and derivative constants may depend on that stage.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-stabilized-contraction",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_M\\ge\\kappa>0$ and take the comparison data of <a href=\"#thm:comparison-data\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:comparison-data\">8</a> at scale $R$, so that $$L_0=J/R,\\qquad \\log q\\le JR.$$ For all sufficiently large $R$, fix these data once and for all. The constant $C(q)$ is independent of $k$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Classifying space** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here $B\Gamma$ is a CW $K(\Gamma,1)$: a connected CW complex with fundamental group $\Gamma$ and contractible universal cover.
- **Rational essentiality** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The manifold is *rationally essential* if $$(c_M)_*[M]\ne0\quad\text{in }H_n(B\Gamma;\mathbb Q),$$ and *rationally inessential* otherwise.
- **Aspherical manifold** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A manifold is *aspherical* if its universal cover is contractible.
- **Enlargeability** ([source](#pm-h-3)): In the later geometric convention, a closed oriented Riemannian $n$-manifold is *enlargeable* if, for every $\varepsilon>0$, some cover with the lifted metric admits an $\varepsilon$-Lipschitz map of nonzero degree to the unit sphere $S^n$ that is constant outside a compact set.
- **Closed manifold** ([source](#sec:reductions)): A closed manifold is compact and has no boundary.
- **Rips probability complex** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For $a\ge0$, let $\mathcal K_a$ be the Rips complex realized as finite probability vectors $t=(t_\gamma)_{\gamma\in\Gamma}$ whose nonempty support has diameter at most $a$.
- **Uniformly controlled metric** ([source](#sec:graph-existence)): A metric $g$ is *uniformly controlled* if it is uniformly equivalent to $g_{\rm ref}$ and its coordinate coefficients have bounded derivatives of every order.
- **Torus-stabilized metric** ([source](#stmt-def-torical-metric)): A *torus-stabilized metric* on a smooth manifold $Y$ is a metric $$\label{eq:stabilized-metric} \mathcal G=g+\sum_{\nu=1}^{b}m_\nu^2\,d\theta_\nu^2 \qquad\text{on }Y\times\mathbb T^b,$$ where $b\geq0$ is finite, $g$ is a smooth metric on $Y$, and the functions $m_\nu:Y\to(0,\infty)$ are smooth.
- **Pass** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Processing all $q$ coordinates once is called a *pass*.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| comparison-scale | Auxiliary word-metric scale | = | $100R$ | Auxiliary spaces depend on the fixed comparison scale. |
| norm-factor | Probability-profile norm comparison factor | ≤ | $3$ | Exponent is chosen for the fixed coordinate count. |
| compact-profile | Profile norm on the compact core | ≤ | $24L$ | Follows from the label distance and norm comparison; existence remains a geometric assertion. |
| triad-radial-low | Radial regular level | > | $100L$ | A regular level is selected within the source interval. |
| triad-radial-high | Radial regular level | < | $101L$ | Regular-level existence uses Sard theory. |
| witness-gap | Radial witness norm | > | $24L$ | The gap follows from the selected radial level and comparison scale. |
| pass-epsilon | Graph height scaling parameter | = | $L/4$ | Input fibre differential and error allowances are positive. |
| pass-regularizer | Slope regularization parameter | = | $q^{-3}$ | Finite coordinate count is fixed for this pass. |
| pass-constant | Sufficient one-coordinate scalar loss constant | = | $128\sqrt2 e^3$ | A sufficient constant, rather than an asserted optimal value. |
| schedule-lipschitz | Fibre row-norm schedule | = | $4^{-j}L_0$ | Finite pass count; initial differential allowance is positive. |
| schedule-error | Coordinate error schedule | = | $2^{-j}/10$ | The geometric schedule has a summable total. |
| iteration-constant | Dimension-dependent sufficient scalar-loss constant | = | $(400/3)C_0(n+1)$ | The coefficient comes from the geometric sum of squared schedule ratios. |
| total-error | Uniform coordinate displacement | < | $\pi/10$ | Finite iteration with a summable coordinate error schedule. |
| scalar-retention | Assembled stabilized scalar curvature | ≥ | $\kappa/2$ | A fixed finite stage is assembled after fibre shortening. |
| full-differential | Full base differential norm | ≤ | $\sqrt{q+1}\,4^{-k}L_0$ | Coordinate count is fixed before selecting the finite pass count. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **probability** [claim](#stmt-lem-probability-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-probability-map): Triangulation, equivariant smoothing, and alternating cocycle detection -> Proper equivariant probability data
- **pairing** [claim](#stmt-prop-compact-pairing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-compact-pairing): Controlled cap operation and cellular duality -> Compactly supported nonzero pairing
- **profiles** [claim](#stmt-lem-distance-profiles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-distance-profiles): Word-metric distance profiles and support comparison -> Proper finite coordinates with controlled fibre derivatives
- **relative-degree** [claim](#stmt-lem-relative-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-relative-degree): Relative duality, rational realization, and transversality -> Parameter cycle with nonzero intersection count
- **comparison** [claim](#stmt-thm-comparison-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-relative-degree-2): Doubling, pullback of the principal cover, cutoff, and scaling -> Nonzero local degree and controlled comparison data
- **scalar-identity** [claim](#stmt-prop-scalar-update) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-scalar-update): Warped-product formulas, graph Jacobi identity, and rank-one inversion -> Scalar update and inverse-metric identities
- **graph** [claim](#stmt-thm-uniform-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-uniform-graph): Continuation, tangent-band barriers, gradient bounds, and uniform elliptic estimates -> Unique graph solution with smooth parameter dependence
- **pass** [claim](#stmt-lem-one-pass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-one-pass): Square absorption and telescoping inverse metrics -> Logarithmic scalar-loss bound per pass
- **shortening** [claim](#stmt-thm-shortening) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-shortening): Summable schedules followed by parameter-metric enlargement -> Positive stabilized scalar bound and contracting full differential
- **obstruction** [claim](#stmt-thm-torical-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-torical-obstruction): Pull back a torus tube, double the band, enlarge circles, and apply the local torical theorem -> Obstruction threshold independent of finite circle count
- **main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-torical-obstruction-2): Fix comparison scale, then choose a finite shortening count contradicting the stabilized obstruction -> Rational inessentiality
- **consequences** [claim](#stmt-cor-simplicial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-torical-obstruction-6): Nonnegative scalar-curvature reduction and simplicial-volume detection -> Zero real simplicial volume

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **rational-vanishing**: Strict positive scalar curvature forces the fundamental class to vanish in rational classifying-space homology.
- **aspherical-rigidity**: Nonnegative scalar curvature forces the given aspherical metric to be flat.
- **metric-separation**: Scalar curvature belongs to the full stabilized metric, whereas the differential of a base map is measured in the base metric.
- **finite-stage-control**: Uniform coefficient and derivative bounds are asserted at each fixed finite stage; the constants may depend on that stage.
- **fixed-scale-order**: Fix the comparison dimension and geometry before varying the finite pass count.
- **parameter-derivative-scope**: Comparison parameter derivatives and higher derivatives are not asserted to have bounds uniform in the comparison scale.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **nonnegative-essentiality**: Replace strict positive scalar curvature by nonnegative scalar curvature in rational inessentiality. -> A positive-dimensional torus is rationally essential and admits a flat metric.
- **engineering-transfer**: Apply the obstruction directly to a discrete graph or an arbitrary engineering state space. -> No closed oriented smooth manifold and admissible Riemannian metric have been established for that object.
- **base-scalar-confusion**: Treat the stabilized scalar lower bound as a scalar lower bound on the base metric. -> The added warped circles enter scalar curvature, so the stated lower bound concerns a different metric.
- **infinite-stage-limit**: Infer convergence to an infinite-stage smooth complete stabilized metric from finite shortening. -> Finite-stage bounds permit constants to vary with the stage and do not assert an infinite-stage limit.
- **scale-uniformity**: Use the comparison construction as a scale-uniform bound for parameter or higher derivatives. -> The comparison statement explicitly withholds that uniformity.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The shortening construction obtains smooth functions by a nonlinear elliptic existence theorem on a Riemannian cover. Its uniform regularity and parameter enlargement are asserted at each fixed finite stage, with constants allowed to depend on that stage. The paper does not supply a finite representation of those geometric inputs or an effective solution and error certificate. Arithmetic checks of the displayed estimates would not certify the manifold, scalar-curvature, or degree conclusions.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): Rational inessentiality requires the stated closed, connected, oriented smooth manifold and strictly positive scalar curvature hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Applying the rational classifying-image conclusion to spaces whose manifold and metric hypotheses have not been established.']
- **L2** (counterexample_scope): Nonnegative scalar curvature cannot replace strict positivity in the rational inessentiality theorem. A flat positive-dimensional torus remains rationally essential. Blocked use: ['Inferring rational inessentiality from a nonnegative scalar curvature metric.']
- **L3** (counterexample_scope): Zero simplicial volume does not imply rational inessentiality. Blocked use: ['Using simplicial-volume vanishing as a converse test for rational essentiality.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): The simplicial-volume conclusion concerns closed oriented smooth manifolds of positive dimension. Blocked use: ['Extending the vanishing conclusion to arbitrary bounded domains or unoriented spaces without a separate argument.']
- **L5** (formal_scope): The classifying-image conclusion uses rational coefficients. Blocked use: ['Inferring vanishing of an integral classifying image or elimination of torsion from the rational conclusion.']
- **L6** (approximation): Comparison-data parameter derivatives and higher derivatives are bounded only after fixing the comparison scale; uniform bounds as the scale varies are not asserted. Blocked use: ['Assuming scale-independent bounds for all mixed derivatives.']
- **L7** (formal_scope): The inverse-metric trace is taken in the original fibre directions, while the sphere-map differential uses the full base. Blocked use: ['Replacing the full differential required by the obstruction with a fibre-only differential bound.']
- **L8** (nonconstructive): Uniform graph solvability is a smooth analytic existence result; it does not supply a finite input representation or an executable decision procedure for curvature obstructions. Blocked use: ['Publishing an algorithm that decides the manifold theorem from finite data without an additional representation and correctness argument.']
- **L9** (formal_scope): Every shortening stage is finite, and its equivalence and derivative constants may depend on that stage. Blocked use: ['Asserting convergence of the metrics to an infinite-stage stabilized metric with uniform regularity.']
- **L10** (formal_scope): The comparison scale and resulting base dimension are fixed before the finite pass count is increased. Blocked use: ['Letting the target dimension vary with the pass count when invoking the fixed-dimension obstruction.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): The stabilized obstruction requires a smooth sphere map of nonzero degree that is constant outside a compact set, with a bound on its full differential. Blocked use: ['Applying the obstruction to arbitrary noncompact maps without the required degree and exterior-value conditions.']
- **L12** (formal_scope): The quantitative scalar-curvature comparison for simplicial volume remains a separate question. Blocked use: ['Claiming a quantitative simplicial-volume versus Riemannian-volume inequality from qualitative vanishing.']
- **L13** (formal_scope): The terminal torical obstruction invokes a published geometric theorem at its stated interface. Blocked use: ['Treating finite arithmetic checks as a certification of the external minimal-slicing input.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every closed connected oriented smooth manifold admitting strictly positive scalar curvature is rationally inessential: its rational fundamental class maps to zero under the classifying map. No spin or fundamental-group hypothesis is needed, and there is no upper dimension bound. This proves the Gromov–Lawson aspherical conjecture; moreover, every nonnegative-scalar-curvature metric on a closed aspherical manifold is flat. We also show that every closed oriented smooth manifold of positive dimension with nonnegative scalar curvature has zero real simplicial volume, proving the qualitative vanishing consequence of Gromov's conjectural comparison.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $M^n$ be a closed connected oriented smooth manifold, let $\Gamma=\pi_1(M)$, and let $c_M:M\to B\Gamma$ be a classifying map for its universal cover. Here $B\Gamma$ is a CW $K(\Gamma,1)$: a connected CW complex with fundamental group $\Gamma$ and contractible universal cover. The manifold is *rationally essential* if $$(c_M)_*[M]\ne0\quad\text{in }H_n(B\Gamma;\mathbb Q),$$ and *rationally inessential* otherwise. The fundamental class in this definition has rational coefficients. The condition is independent of the choice of classifying map. It records whether the fundamental class remains visible after passing from the manifold to the topology of its fundamental group.

We prove the rational-essentiality obstruction to positive scalar curvature formulated in (Hanke 2012, Conjecture 2.9) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Hanke2011](#ref-Hanke2011)].

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Rational inessentiality). *Let $M^n$ be a closed connected oriented smooth manifold of dimension $n\ge2$. If $M$ admits a Riemannian metric of strictly positive scalar curvature, then $$(c_M)_*[M]=0\qquad\text{in }H_n(B\pi_1(M);\mathbb Q).$$*

</div>

The theorem has no spin, universal-cover spin, or fundamental-group hypothesis, and no upper dimension bound. In particular, the group may have torsion and its classifying space need not have a finite-dimensional model.

A manifold is *aspherical* if its universal cover is contractible.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### From a detected class to contracting maps

In the later geometric convention, a closed oriented Riemannian $n$-manifold is *enlargeable* if, for every $\varepsilon>0$, some cover with the lifted metric admits an $\varepsilon$-Lipschitz map of nonzero degree to the unit sphere $S^n$ that is constant outside a compact set. This definition imposes no spin condition. Rational essentiality supplies a group-cohomology class whose pullback pairs nontrivially with $[M]$, but it does not guarantee these contracting maps. Brunnbauer and Hanke construct rationally essential manifolds which are not enlargeable, as well as enlargeable manifolds whose universal covers alone do not admit such maps at every scale, that is, are not hyperspherical (Brunnbauer and Hanke 2010, Theorems 1.4–1.5) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[BrunnbauerHanke2010](#ref-BrunnbauerHanke2010)]. Their examples are not aspherical.

Guth and Braun–Sauer give a different comparison between topology and metric largeness: at every radius, they guarantee a ball in the universal cover whose volume is at least a dimensional multiple of the radius to the $n$th power, for closed aspherical and rationally essential manifolds, respectively (Guth 2011; Braun and Sauer 2021) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Guth2011](#ref-Guth2011), [BraunSauer2021](#ref-BraunSauer2021)]. These bounds do not have the sharp Euclidean leading constant, so they do not determine the scalar-curvature term in the small-ball volume expansion.

Our proof converts the cohomology pairing into a local degree by adding a parameter manifold, then changes the metric to contract the resulting map while retaining positive scalar curvature. The main estimate makes the scalar cost grow only logarithmically with the number of comparison coordinates. Suppose for contradiction that $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_M\ge\kappa>0$ and that $M$ is rationally essential. The construction has four stages.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### Finite comparison and local degree.

At each sufficiently large finite word-metric comparison scale $R$, an auxiliary closed oriented manifold $C$ with a principal left $\Gamma$-cover $\widehat C\to C$ gives the cover $X=(\widetilde M\times\widehat C)/\Gamma\to M\times C$. The detecting cocycle yields a compactly supported cohomology class on $X$ that evaluates nontrivially on its locally finite fundamental class. Distance profiles supply a preliminary map $\mathcal V:X\to\mathbb R^q$, for a finite integer $q>n$. Together with the parameter projection $\bar o:X\to C$, it defines the map $x\mapsto(\bar o(x),\mathcal V(x))$ into $C\times\mathbb R^q$.

<a id="eq:introduction-data"></a>
The construction transfers the compact pairing to a relative cohomology class on a compact region of this product, evaluated nontrivially on a relative cycle carried by the preceding map. Relative duality gives a complementary relative rational homology class, and rational realization represents a nonzero multiple by a compact oriented $(q-n)$-manifold with boundary mapped to $C\times\mathbb R^q$. Its transverse intersections with the preceding map have nonzero signed count. Doubling the parameter manifold and reflecting its map gives a closed oriented manifold $A$ with a map $(o_0,y_0):A\to C\times\mathbb R^q$. Pulling back $\widehat C\to C$ along $o_0$ gives a principal cover $\widehat A\to A$ and the fibre product $$Z=X\times_C A=(\widetilde M\times\widehat A)/\Gamma
       \longrightarrow M\times A.$$ This cover has dimension $q=n+\dim A$, and both $\mathcal V$ and $y_0$ pull back to $\mathbb R^q$-valued maps on it. On a precompact open set $\Omega$ over one half of $A$, the zeros of their scaled difference $(\mathcal V-y_0)/R$ are precisely the preceding intersections. A cutoff equal to one on $\Omega$ makes this difference a smooth compactly supported map $G:Z\to\mathbb R^q$ satisfying $$\deg(G,\Omega,0)\ne0,
 \qquad \|G\|_\infty>3\quad\text{on }\partial\Omega,$$ where $\|\cdot\|_\infty$ is the maximum of the absolute target coordinates. The scalar coordinates satisfy $$\label{eq:introduction-data}
 \max_i|d_{\widetilde M}G_i|\le J/R,
 \qquad \log q\le JR,$$ with $J$ independent of $R$. Here $d_{\widetilde M}$ differentiates in the fibre directions, holding the parameter in $A$ fixed, and its norm uses the lifted metric of $M$. Parameter derivatives are bounded at each fixed $R$ and are controlled later by metric enlargement. [sec:comparison](#sec:comparison) constructs these data without assuming that $\widetilde M$ is contractible or that the cocycle is bounded.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### A graph and one warped circle per coordinate.

A graph change increases the fibre metric in the direction of one function’s differential, and a reciprocal warped circle supplies favorable scalar terms. A nonlinear elliptic equation chooses a replacement coordinate close to the old one while shortening its differential. Processing all $q$ coordinates once is called a *pass*. Paying an independent worst-case scalar cost for every coordinate would introduce a factor $q$ and destroy the scalar lower bound. Instead, the decreases of the inverse metric telescope on the $n$-dimensional fibre cotangent space. Their sum has trace at most $n$ in the metric at the start of the pass, independently of the numbers of coordinates and circles. The scalar identity in <a href="#sec:graph-calculus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:graph-calculus">4</a>, the graph solution in <a href="#sec:graph-existence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:graph-existence">5</a>, and the summation in <a href="#sec:shortening" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:shortening">6</a> bound the fibre scalar-curvature loss by $C(n+1)(2+\log q)L^2/w^2$, for an absolute constant $C$, when the input row norms are at most $L$ and each replacement is allowed coordinate error $\pi w/2$.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Finite shortening with a summable cost.

Successive passes divide $L$ by four and $w$ by two, so their coordinate errors and fibre scalar losses form summable series. The initial error allowance makes the uniform total error in each coordinate less than $\pi/10$, preserving the maximum-norm boundary gap. Composing the shortened coordinate map on $\Omega$ with a degree-one map $\mathbb R^q\to S^q$ that is constant outside the unit ball, and extending constantly outside $\Omega$, preserves this degree. By <a href="#eq:introduction-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:introduction-data">[eq:introduction-data]</a>, the total fibre scalar loss is $O((2+\log q)R^{-2})=O(R^{-1})$. At each fixed finite stage, enlarging the metric on $A$ makes the parameter part of the differential small and makes the assembled scalar curvature uniformly close to the fibre scalar curvature. For $R$ sufficiently large and every finite integer $k\ge1$, there are a metric $g_k$ on $Z$, positive warps $m_{k,\nu}$, and a smooth map $F_k:Z\to S^q$, constant outside a compact set and of nonzero degree, such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_{g_k+\sum_{\nu=1}^{kq}m_{k,\nu}^2d\theta_\nu^2}\ge\kappa/2,
 \qquad
 |dF_k|_{g_k,\mathrm{op}}\le C(q)4^{-k}J/R.$$ The scalar bound concerns $Z\times\mathbb T^{kq}$, while the differential bound uses all the base directions in $Z$. [fig:direction-splitting](#fig:direction-splitting) distinguishes these directions from the stabilizing circles.

<figure id="fig:direction-splitting">

<figcaption>The local direction splitting after $k$ finite passes. The base $Z$ is locally a product of original and parameter directions; no global product decomposition is asserted. The inverse-metric trace uses only the $n$ original directions. The sphere map uses the full base of dimension $q$, which is fixed before the number $kq$ of circles is chosen.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-direction-splitting"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:direction-splitting`)
